Appendix F
Proofs

In what follows, for a random variable
X
and
\nu\geq 1
, we denote
\|X\|_{\nu}=(\mathsf{E}|X|^{\nu})^{1/\nu}
.

We work under the following assumption which extends Assumption
2.4
by considering the special case of Gaussianity separately.

Assumption F.1
.

(i)

\{\mathbf{u}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a sequence of zero-mean,
q
-dimensional martingale difference vectors with
\mathsf{Cov}(\mathbf{u}_{t})=\mathbf{I}_{q}
, and
u_{it}
and
u_{jt}
are independent for all
1\leq i,j\leq q
with
i\neq j
and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Also,
\{\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a sequence of zero-mean,
p
-dimensional martingale difference vectors with
\mathsf{Cov}(\bm{\varepsilon}_{t})=\mathbf{I}_{p}
, and
\varepsilon_{it}
and
\varepsilon_{jt}
are independent for all
1\leq i,j\leq p
with
i\neq j
and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(ii)

The common and idiosyncratic shocks are uncorrelated, i.e.
\mathsf{E}(u_{jt}\varepsilon_{it^{\prime}})=0
for any
1\leq i\leq p
,
1\leq j\leq q
and
t,t^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(iii)

Either one of the following conditions holds.

(a)

There exists
\nu>4
such that

\max\left\{\max_{1\leq j\leq q}\|u_{jt}\|_{\nu}^{\nu},\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\|\varepsilon_{it}\|_{\nu}^{\nu})\right\}\leq\mu_{\nu}

for some constant
\mu_{\nu}>0
.

(b)

\mathbf{u}_{t}\sim_{\text{iid}}\mathcal{N}_{q}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I})
and

\bm{\varepsilon}_{t}\sim_{\text{iid}}\mathcal{N}_{p}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I})
.

Accordingly, we define
\psi_{n}
and
\vartheta_{n,p}
as in (
10
)
under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(a)
, while

\displaystyle\psi_{n}=\sqrt{\frac{m\log(m)}{n}}\quad\text{and}\quad\vartheta_{n,p}=\sqrt{\frac{m\log(mp)}{n}}

(F.1)

under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(b)
.

F.1
Proof of Proposition
2.1

Let
\mathcal{D}(z)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{D}_{\ell}z^{\ell}
.
Under Assumption
2.3
, we can find a constant
B_{\xi}>0

which depends only on
M_{\varepsilon}
,
\Xi
and
\varsigma

such that, uniformly over
\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\xi,1}(\omega)=

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Sigma}_{\xi}(\omega)\|=\frac{1}{2\pi}\|\mathcal{D}(e^{-\iota\omega})\bm{\Gamma}\mathcal{D}^{*}(e^{-\iota\omega})\|\leq\frac{M_{\varepsilon}}{2\pi}\|\mathcal{D}(e^{-\iota\omega})\|_{1}\;\|\mathcal{D}(e^{-\iota\omega})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\varepsilon}}{2\pi}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|D_{\ell,ij}|\right)\left(\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|D_{\ell,ij}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{M_{\varepsilon}}{2\pi}\left(\max_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{ij}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}}\right)\left(\max_{j}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{ij}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}}\right)\leq\frac{\Xi^{2}M_{\varepsilon}}{2\pi}\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}}\right)^{2}\leq B_{\xi}.

F.2
Results in Section
3.5

F.2.1
Preliminary lemmas

In the following lemmas, we operate
under Assumptions
2.2
,
2.1
,
2.3
and
F.1
.

Zhang and Wu, 2021
extend the functional dependence measure
introduced in
Wu, 2005
for high-dimensional time series.
Denote by
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\sigma\{(\mathbf{u}_{v},\bm{\varepsilon}_{v}),\,v\leq t\}

and
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)=(g_{1}(\cdot),\ldots,g_{p}(\cdot))^{\top}

a
\mathbb{R}^{p}
-valued measurable function such that

\mathbf{X}_{t}=\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{F}_{t})
and
X_{it}=g_{i}(\mathcal{F}_{t})
.
Also let
\mathcal{F}_{t,\{0\}}=\sigma\{\ldots,(\mathbf{u}_{-1},\bm{\varepsilon}_{-1}),(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{0},\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{0}),(\mathbf{u}_{1},\bm{\varepsilon}_{1})^{\top},\ldots,(\mathbf{u}_{t},\bm{\varepsilon}_{t})\}

denote a coupled version of
\mathcal{F}_{t}
with an independent copy

(\mathbf{u}^{\prime}_{0},\bm{\varepsilon}^{\prime}_{0})
replacing
(\mathbf{u}_{0},\bm{\varepsilon}_{0})
.
Then, the element-wise functional dependence measure is defined as

\displaystyle\delta_{t,\nu,i}=\left\|g_{i}(\mathcal{F}_{t})-g_{i}(\mathcal{F}_{t,\{0\}})\right\|_{\nu},

the uniform functional dependence measure as

\displaystyle\delta_{t,\nu}=\left\||\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{F}_{t})-\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{F}_{t,\{0\}})|_{\infty}\right\|_{\nu},

the dependence adjusted norms as

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}_{i\cdot}\|_{\nu,\alpha}=\sup_{\ell\geq 0}\,(\ell+1)^{\alpha}\sum_{t=\ell}^{\infty}\delta_{t,\nu,i}\quad\text{and}\quad\||\mathbf{X}_{\cdot}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu,\alpha}=\sup_{\ell\geq 0}\,(\ell+1)^{\alpha}\sum_{t=\ell}^{\infty}\delta_{t,\nu},

and the overall and the uniform dependence adjusted norms as

\displaystyle\Psi_{\nu,\alpha}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{p}\|\mathbf{X}_{i\cdot}\|_{\nu,\alpha}^{\nu/2}\right)^{2/\nu}\quad\text{and}\quad\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}=\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\|\mathbf{X}_{i\cdot}\|_{\nu,\alpha}.

Lemma F.1
.

Let
\alpha\leq\varsigma-1
.

(i)

Under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(a)
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}p^{2/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\quad\text{and}\quad\||\mathbf{X}_{\cdot}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(p)p^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

for some constant
C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}>0
depending only on
\nu
and
\varsigma

(varying on each occasion).

(ii)

Under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(a)
–
(iii)(b)
, we have

\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

for any
\nu
for which
\|u_{jt}\|_{\nu}
and
\|\varepsilon_{it}\|_{\nu}
exist.

Proof.

By Minkowski inequality,

\displaystyle\delta_{t,\nu,i}

\displaystyle=\|\mathbf{B}_{t,i\cdot}\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{\nu}\leq\|\mathbf{B}_{t,i\cdot}\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{\nu}+\|\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{\nu},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\delta_{t,\nu}

\displaystyle=\||\mathbf{B}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{0}+\mathbf{D}_{t}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}\leq\||\mathbf{B}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}+\||\mathbf{D}_{t}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}.

Due to independence of
u_{jt},\,1\leq j\leq q
, assumed in Assumption
F.1

(i)
, Assumption
2.2
and Lemma D.3 of
Zhang and Wu, 2021
, there exists
C_{\nu}>0
depends only on
\nu

(varying from one instance to another) such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{B}_{t,i\cdot}\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{\nu}=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{q}B_{t,ij}u_{j0}\right\|_{\nu}\leq C_{\nu}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{q}\|B_{t,ij}u_{j0}\|_{\nu}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq C_{\nu}|\mathbf{B}_{t,i\cdot}|_{2}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\leq C_{\nu}\Xi(1+t)^{-\varsigma}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

for all
1\leq i\leq p
, and

\displaystyle\||\mathbf{B}_{t}\mathbf{u}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}\leq C_{\nu}\log^{1/2}(p)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{q}|\mathbf{B}_{t,\cdot k}|_{\infty}^{2}\right)^{1/2}q^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\leq C_{\nu}\log^{1/2}(p)\Xi(1+t)^{-\varsigma}q^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}.

Similarly, from Assumption
2.3
and independence of
\varepsilon_{it}
assumed in Assumption
F.1

(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{\nu}\leq C_{\nu}|\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}|_{2}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\leq C_{\nu}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}|\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}|_{2}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{p}C_{ik}^{2}\right)^{1/2}(1+t)^{-\varsigma}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\leq C_{\nu}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\Xi(1+t)^{-\varsigma}\;\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

for all
1\leq i\leq p
.
Then,

\displaystyle\||\mathbf{D}_{t}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}^{\nu}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\|\mathbf{D}_{t,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}\|_{\nu}^{\nu}\leq p(C_{\nu}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\Xi(1+t)^{-\varsigma})^{\nu}\mu_{\nu}

(F.2)

such that

\||\mathbf{D}_{t}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{0}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu}\leq C_{\nu}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\Xi(1+t)^{-\varsigma}p^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}
.
Then, for some constant
C_{\nu,\Xi}>0
,

\displaystyle\delta_{t,\nu,i}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}(1+t)^{-\varsigma}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu},\quad\delta_{t,\nu}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(p)(1+t)^{-\varsigma}p^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}

and setting
\alpha\leq\varsigma-1
leads to

\displaystyle\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu},\quad\Psi_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}p^{2/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\||\mathbf{X}_{\cdot}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu,\alpha}\leq C_{\nu,\Xi,\varsigma}M_{\varepsilon}^{1/2}\log^{1/2}(p)p^{1/\nu}\mu_{\nu}^{1/\nu}.

∎

Lemma F.2
.

Denote by
\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)=[\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell),\,1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p]
.
Then, there exists some constant
C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}

depending only on
\Xi,\varsigma
defined in Assumptions
2.2
and
2.3

(iv)
and
M_{\varepsilon}
, such that

\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\leq C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}(1+|\ell|)^{-\varsigma}

and consequently,

\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sum_{|\ell|>m}\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)=O(m^{-\varsigma+1})=o(m^{-1})
.

Proof.

By Assumption
F.1

(ii)
,
we have
\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)=\bm{\Gamma}_{\chi}(\ell)+\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(\ell)
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
and define
\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)
and
\gamma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)

analogously as
\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)
.
From Assumption
2.2
, for some
h\geq 0
,

\displaystyle|\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(h)|=\left|\mathsf{E}\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{q}B_{\ell,ij}B_{\ell^{\prime},i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}u_{j,t-\ell-h}u_{j^{\prime},t-\ell^{\prime}}\right)\right|=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{q}B_{\ell,ij}B_{\ell+h,i^{\prime}j}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}|\mathbf{B}_{\ell,i\cdot}|_{2}\;|\mathbf{B}_{\ell+h,i^{\prime}\cdot}|_{2}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Xi^{2}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}(1+\ell+h)^{\varsigma}}\leq\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Xi^{2}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}(1+h)^{\varsigma}}\leq C_{\Xi,\varsigma}(1+h)^{-\varsigma}

(F.3)

uniformly in
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
for some
C_{\Xi,\varsigma}>0

depending only on
\Xi
and
\varsigma
. Therefore,

\sum_{|\ell|>m}|\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)|\leq 2C_{\Xi,\varsigma}\sum_{\ell>m}\ell^{-\varsigma}=O(m^{-\varsigma+1})
.
Similarly, from Assumption
2.3

(iv)
,

\displaystyle|\gamma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(h)|=

\displaystyle\left|\mathsf{E}\left(\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=0}^{\infty}(\mathbf{D}_{\ell,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-\ell-h})(\mathbf{D}_{\ell^{\prime},i^{\prime}\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\bm{\varepsilon}_{t-\ell^{\prime}})\right)\right|=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\left|\mathbf{D}_{\ell,i\cdot}\bm{\Gamma}\mathbf{D}^{\top}_{\ell+h,i^{\prime}\cdot}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\|\bm{\Gamma}\||\mathbf{D}_{\ell,i\cdot}|_{2}\;|\mathbf{D}_{\ell+h,i^{\prime}\cdot}|_{2}\leq M_{\varepsilon}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Xi^{2}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}(1+\ell+h)^{\varsigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Xi^{2}}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}(1+h)^{\varsigma}}\leq C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}(1+h)^{-\varsigma}

(F.4)

uniformly in
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p

for some
C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}>0

depending only on
\Xi
,
\varsigma
and
M_{\varepsilon}
.
Therefore,

\sum_{|\ell|>m}|\gamma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)|=O(m^{-\varsigma+1})
,
which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.3
.

Denote by
\bm{\Sigma}_{x}(\omega)=[\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega),1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p]
.
Then, there exists a constant
B_{\sigma}>0
such that

\sup_{\pi\in[-\pi,\pi]}\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\leq B_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
F.2
, we can find
B_{\sigma}
that depends only on
\Xi
,
\varsigma
and
\bm{\Gamma}

such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{\infty}\left|\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|\leq\frac{C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}}{2\pi}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+|\ell|)^{\varsigma}}\leq B_{\sigma}.

∎

Lemma F.4
.

For all
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
, the functions
\omega\mapsto\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)

possess derivatives of any order and are of bounded variation,
i.e. there exists
B^{\prime}_{\sigma}>0
such that

\sum_{k=1}^{N}|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{n})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k-1})|\leq B^{\prime}_{\sigma}

uniformly in
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and any partition of
[-\pi,\pi]
,

-\pi=\omega_{0}<\omega_{1}<\ldots<\omega_{N}=\pi
.

Proof.

From Lemma
F.2
,

\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}|\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)|\leq C_{\Xi,\varsigma}(1+|\ell|)^{-\varsigma}

for all
\ell
, which implies that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{\infty}\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)e^{-\iota\omega\ell}

has derivatives of all orders. Moreover,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{d}{d\omega}\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{\infty}\left|(-\iota\ell)\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)e^{-\iota\omega\ell}\right|\leq\frac{C_{\Xi,\varsigma}}{\pi}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\ell}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}}\leq C^{\prime}_{\Xi,\varsigma}

for some constant
C^{\prime}_{\Xi,\varsigma}>0
not depending on
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
or
\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]
,
which entails the bounded variation of
\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)
.
∎

Lemma F.5
.

Let
\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)=[\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)]_{i,i^{\prime}=1}^{p}

and
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(\ell)=[\widehat{\gamma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)]_{i,i^{\prime}=1}^{p}
.
For some fixed
s\in\mathbb{N}
, the following holds.

(i)

p^{-1}\max_{-s\leq\ell\leq s}\|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(\ell)-\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)\|_{F}=O_{P}(n^{-1/2})
.

(ii)

\max_{-s\leq\ell\leq s}|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(\ell)-\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)|_{\infty}=O_{P}(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p})
where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{p^{2/\nu}\log^{3}(p)}{n^{1-2/\nu}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(p)}{n}}&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:moment},}\\
\sqrt{\frac{\log(p)}{n}}&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:gauss},}\end{array}\right.

see also (
12
).

Proof.

In Lemma
F.1
,
for
\varsigma>2
, we can always set
\alpha=\varsigma-1>1/2-2/\nu
.
By Proposition 3.3 of
Zhang and Wu, 2021
,
there exist universal constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0

and constants
C_{\alpha},C_{\nu,\alpha}>0
that depend only on their subscripts,
such that for any
z>0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\left(\max_{-s\leq\ell\leq s}\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|>z\right)\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}ns^{\nu/4}\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}^{\nu}}{(nz)^{\nu/2}}+C_{1}s\exp\left(-\frac{nz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:moment}},\\
2s\exp\left[-C_{2}\min\left(\frac{nz^{2}}{\Phi_{2,0}^{4}},\frac{nz}{\Phi_{2,0}^{2}}\right)\right]&\text{under~Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:gauss}.}\end{array}\right.

Then, due to Lemma
F.1
, the finiteness of
s
and since
\nu>4
under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(a)
,
there exists some constant
C>0
not dependent on
i,i^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\max_{-s\leq\ell\leq s}\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell))\right|^{2}\right)\leq\frac{C}{n}

(F.5)

and
(i)
follows by Chebyshev’s inequality (see also Remark
F.1
).
By the same proposition and Lemma
F.1
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\left(\max_{-s\leq\ell\leq s}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(\ell)-\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(\ell)\right|_{\infty}>z\right)\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}ns^{\nu/4}(\log(p)\||\mathbf{X}_{\cdot}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu,\alpha}\wedge\Psi_{\nu,\alpha})^{\nu}}{(nz)^{\nu/2}}+C_{1}sp^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{nz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:moment}},\\
2sp^{2}\exp\left[-C_{2}\min\left(\frac{nz^{2}}{\Phi_{2,0}^{4}},\frac{nz}{\Phi_{2,0}^{2}}\right)\right]&\text{under~Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:gauss},}\end{array}\right.

from which
(ii)
follows.
∎

In what follows, we assume that
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}=[\widehat{s}_{ii^{\prime}},\,1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p]
is an estimator of
a Hermitian, positive semi-definite matrix
\mathbf{S}\in\mathbb{C}^{p\times p}
of a finite rank
q<p
.
In addition, we have
|\mathbf{S}|_{\infty}=O(1)
.
Denoting the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
\mathbf{S}
by

(\mu_{j},\mathbf{e}_{j}),\,1\leq j\leq q
, we assume that there exist
pairs of constants
(\alpha_{j},\beta_{j})
and constants
\rho_{j}\in(0,1]

with
\rho_{1}\geq\ldots\geq\rho_{q}
satisfying

\displaystyle\beta_{1}\geq\frac{\mu_{1}}{p^{\rho_{1}}}\geq\alpha_{1}>\beta_{2}\geq\frac{\mu_{2}}{p^{\rho_{2}}}\geq\alpha_{2}>\ldots>\beta_{q}\geq\frac{\mu_{q}}{p^{\rho_{q}}}\geq\alpha_{q}>0.

(F.6)

Also, we assume that there exist a (deterministic) matrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}=[\widetilde{s}_{i,i^{\prime}},\,1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p]
,
a constant
C>0
not dependent on
i,i^{\prime}
and

\zeta_{n,p},\bar{\zeta}_{n,p}\to 0
as
n,p\to 0
, such that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|

\displaystyle=O(1),

(F.7)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}[(\widehat{s}_{ii^{\prime}}-\widetilde{s}_{ii^{\prime}})^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C(\zeta_{n,p})^{2},

(F.8)

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}|\widehat{s}_{ii^{\prime}}-\widetilde{s}_{ii^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle=O_{P}(\bar{\zeta}_{n,p}).

(F.9)

Finally, we assume that
p^{1-\rho_{q}}\zeta_{n,p}\to 0
as
n,p\to\infty
.
Denoting the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
\mathbf{S}
by

(\widehat{\mu}_{j},\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{j}),\,j\geq 1
,
let
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}=[\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{j},\,1\leq j\leq q]

and
\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}=\text{diag}(\widehat{\mu}_{j},\,1\leq j\leq q)

and analogously define
\mathbf{E}
and
\bm{\mathcal{M}}
with
(\mu_{j},\mathbf{e}_{j})
.

Lemma F.6
.

There exists a unitary, diagonal matrix

\bm{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathbb{C}^{q\times q}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right\|_{F}=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

Proof.

By (
F.7
), (
F.8
) and Chebyshev’s inequality,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|\leq\frac{1}{p}\ \|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}\|+\frac{1}{p}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|\leq\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}\|_{F}+\frac{1}{p}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

(F.10)

Then by Theorem 2 of
Yu et al., 2015
,
there exist such
\bm{\mathcal{O}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\|_{F}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2q}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|}{\min(\mu_{0}-\mu_{1},\mu_{q})}

with
\mu_{0}=\infty
which, combined with (
F.10
), concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.7
.

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{q}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

Proof.

As a consequence of (
F.10
) and Weyl’s inequality,
for all
1\leq j\leq q
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}|\widehat{\mu}_{j}-\mu_{j}|\leq\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

(F.11)

Also from (
F.6
),
there exists
\alpha_{q}
such that

p^{-1}\mu_{q}\geq p^{\rho_{q}-1}\alpha_{q}
and
thus
p^{-1}\widehat{\mu}_{q}\geq p^{\rho_{q}-1}\alpha_{q}+O_{P}(\zeta_{n,p}\vee p^{-1})=p^{\rho_{q}-1}\alpha_{q}(1+o_{P}(1))
,
which implies that the matrix
p^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{M}}
is invertible and
the inverse of
p^{-1}\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}
exists for large enough
n
and
p
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\frac{p}{\mu_{q}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\frac{p}{\widehat{\mu}_{q}}=\frac{1}{p^{-1}\mu_{q}(1+o_{P}(1))}.

Then from (
F.11
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{F}=\sqrt{p^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\left(\frac{1}{\widehat{\mu}_{j}}-\frac{1}{\mu_{j}}\right)^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\left(\frac{p^{-1}(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-\mu_{j})}{p^{-1}\widehat{\mu}_{j}\cdot p^{-1}\mu_{j}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{q}\left(\frac{p^{-1}(\widehat{\mu}_{j}-\mu_{j})}{(p^{-1}\mu_{j})^{2}(1+o_{P}(1))}\right)^{2}}=O_{P}\left(\frac{\sqrt{q}p}{\mu_{q}}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right)\right).

The second claim follows similarly.
∎

Lemma F.8
.

Let
\bm{\varphi}_{i}
denote the
p
-vector whose
i
-th element is one
and the rest are set to be zero. Then, with
\bm{\mathcal{O}}
defined in Lemma
F.6
,

(i)

uniformly for all
1\leq i\leq p
(in the sense described in Remark
F.1
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\cdot\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)\right|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

(ii)

In addition,

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\cdot\frac{\mu_{q}}{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)\right|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\bar{\zeta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

Proof.

By (
F.7
), (
F.8
) and Chebyshev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S})\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{S}})\right|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)

(F.12)

uniformly in
1\leq i\leq p
.
Then, by (
F.12
), (
F.6
),
Lemmas
F.6
–
F.7
and the finiteness of the elements of
\mathbf{S}
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\cdot\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}})\right|_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\cdot\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left[\widehat{\mathbf{S}}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}-\mathbf{S}\mathbf{E}\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right]\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{q}}{p}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S})\right|_{2}\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|\right.+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\right|_{2}\,\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle+\left.\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\right|_{2}\,\left\|\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|\,\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{E}}-\mathbf{E}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right\|\right\}=O_{P}\left(\zeta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)

uniformly over
1\leq i\leq p
, and thus
(i)
follows.
The claim
(ii)
follows analogously, except that (
F.12
) is replaced by

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S})\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{S}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{S}})\right|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}-\mathbf{S}\|=O_{P}\left(\bar{\zeta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right),

where the inequality follows from (
F.7
) and (
F.9
).
∎

F.2.2
Proof of Theorem
3.1

In what follows, by
\mathbf{e}_{x,j}(\omega)
, we denote the eigenvectors of
\bm{\Sigma}_{x}(\omega)

corresponding to the eigenvalues
\mu_{x,j}(\omega)
,
and we similarly define
\mathbf{e}_{\chi,j}(\omega)
and
\mathbf{e}_{\xi,j}(\omega)
.

Recall the decomposition of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\chi}(\omega)
as

\displaystyle\bm{\Sigma}_{\chi}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\bm{\mathsf{E}}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathsf{E}}_{\chi}^{*}(\omega)=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mu_{\chi,j}(\omega)\mathbf{e}_{\chi,j}(\omega)\mathbf{e}_{\chi,j}^{*}(\omega),

where
\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)
denotes the diagonal matrix with
\mu_{\chi,1}(\omega)\geq\ldots\geq\mu_{\chi,q}(\omega)
, the
q
eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\chi}(\omega)
, on its diagonal, and
\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)=[\mathbf{e}_{\chi,j}(\omega),\,1\leq j\leq q]

the matrix of the normalised eigenvectors associated with
\mu_{\chi,j}(\omega),\,1\leq j\leq q
.
In what follows, we assume the conditions made in Theorem
3.1
are met
and prove a series of results results.
Then, Theorem
3.1
is a direct consequence of Proposition
F.16

(ii)
.

Proposition F.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
not dependent on
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p

such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|^{2}\right)\leq C\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\right)^{2}.

Proof.

Noting that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathsf{E}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|^{2}\right)+2\sup_{\omega}\left|\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))-\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|^{2},

(F.13)

we first address the first term in the RHS of (
F.13
).
In Lemma
F.1
,
for
\varsigma>2
, we can always set
\alpha=\varsigma-1>1/2-2/\nu
.
Then, from the finiteness of
\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}
shown therein and
by Proposition 4.3 of
Zhang and Wu, 2021
,
there exist universal constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0

and constants
C_{\alpha},C_{\nu,\alpha}>0
that depend only on their subscripts,
such that for any
z>0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|>z\right)\leq

(F.14)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}nm^{\nu/2}\Phi_{\nu,\alpha}^{\nu}}{(nz)^{\nu/2}}+C_{1}m\exp\left(-\frac{nz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}m\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:moment}},\\
C_{2}m\exp\left[-C_{3}\min\left(\frac{nz^{2}}{m\Phi_{2,0}^{4}},\frac{nz}{m\Phi_{2,0}^{2}}\right)\right]&\text{under~Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:gauss}.}\end{array}\right.

Noting that for any positive random variable
Y
, we have
\mathsf{E}(Y)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}(Y>y)dy
,
we have

\mathsf{E}(\sup_{\omega}|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))|^{2})\leq C\psi_{n}^{2}
for some constant
C>0
independent of
i,i^{\prime}
(see Remark
F.1
).
Turning our attention to the second term in the RHS of (
F.13
),

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sup_{\omega}2\pi\left|\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))-\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sum_{\ell=-m}^{m}\frac{|\ell|}{m}\left|\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|+\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sum_{|\ell|>m}\left|\gamma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=I+II.

From Lemma
F.2
and that
\varsigma>2
, there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}>0

such that

\displaystyle I\leq 2C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\frac{\ell}{m(1+\ell)^{\varsigma}}\leq\frac{2C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}}{m}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\frac{1}{(1+\ell)^{\varsigma-1}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}}{m}

and
II=O(m^{-\varsigma+1})
, thus
I+II=O(m^{-1})
uniformly in
i,i^{\prime}
and
\omega
,
which completes the proof.
∎

Remark F.1
.

All the probabilistic statements leading to the Frobenius norm bound in Theorem
3.1

are due to Proposition
F.9
, which allows for deriving non-asymptotic bounds.
As in
Forni et al., 2017
, in what follows, we state that
|a_{i,i^{\prime}}|=O_{P}(\psi_{n})

uniformly
in
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
when for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
\eta(\epsilon)>0

independent of
i,i^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\left(\psi_{n}^{-1}|a_{i,i^{\prime}}|>\eta(\epsilon)\right)<\epsilon\quad\text{for all $n$ and $p$}.

Then, since
\psi_{n}^{-2}\mathsf{E}(|a_{i,i^{\prime}}|^{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}(\psi_{n}^{-2}|a_{i,i^{\prime}}|^{2}>z)dz
,
we interchangeably write that there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
i,i^{\prime}
such that

\mathsf{E}(|a_{i,i^{\prime}}|^{2})\leq C\psi_{n}^{2}
for all
n
and
p
.
To see this, let
t=C\psi_{n}
with
\psi_{n}
defined as in Theorem
3.1
.
Then, under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(a)
,
there exists large enough constants
C,C^{\prime}>0

that do not depend on
i,i^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|^{2}\right)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|^{2}\geq z\right)dz

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t^{2}}\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|^{2}\geq z\right)dz+\int_{t^{2}}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|^{2}\geq z\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq t^{2}+\int_{t^{2}}^{\infty}\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega))\right|\geq\sqrt{z}\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq t^{2}+\int_{t^{2}}^{\infty}\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}nm^{\nu/2}\Phi^{\nu}_{\nu,\alpha}}{(n\sqrt{z})^{\nu/2}}dz+\int_{t^{2}}^{\infty}C_{1}m\exp\left(-\frac{nz}{C_{\alpha}m\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)dz

\displaystyle=t^{2}+\left[-\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}n^{1-\nu/2}m^{\nu/2}\Phi^{\nu}_{\nu,\alpha}}{(\nu/4-1)}z^{-\nu/4+1}\right]_{t^{2}}^{\infty}+\left[-\frac{C_{1}C_{\alpha}\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}m^{2}}{n}\exp\left(-\frac{nz}{C_{\alpha}m\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)\right]_{t^{2}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}\Phi^{\nu}_{\nu,\alpha}}{(\nu/4-1)C^{\nu/2}}+\frac{C_{1}C_{\alpha}\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}m^{1-C^{2}/(C_{\alpha}\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4})}}{C^{2}\log(m)}\right)t^{2}\leq C^{\prime}t^{2},

where we use (
F.14
) and that
t=C(mn^{-1+2/\nu}\vee\sqrt{m\log(m)/n})
under the assumption.
The case under Assumption
F.1

(iii)

(iii)(b)

is handled analogously.
In the remainder of the paper, we continue to write
the boundedness in mean square interchangeably with
the
O_{P}
bound where similar arguments apply due to
the upper bound on the tail probability.

Proposition F.10
.

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)-\bm{\Sigma}_{x}(\omega)\right|_{\infty}=O_{P}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\right).

Proof.

In Lemma
F.1
,
for
\varsigma>2
, we can always set
\alpha=\varsigma-1>1/2-2/\nu
.
Then, by Proposition 4.3 of
Zhang and Wu, 2021
,
there exist universal constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0

and constants
C_{\alpha},C_{\nu,\alpha}>0
that depend only on their subscripts,
such that for any
z>0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}\left(\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega))\right|_{\infty}>z\right)\leq

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{C_{\nu,\alpha}nm^{\nu/2}(\log^{5/4}(p)\||\mathbf{X}_{\cdot}|_{\infty}\|_{\nu,\alpha}\wedge\Psi_{\nu,\alpha})^{\nu}}{(nz)^{\nu/2}}+C_{1}mp^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{nz^{2}}{C_{\alpha}m\Phi_{4,\alpha}^{4}}\right)&\text{under Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:moment}},\\
C_{2}mp^{2}\exp\left[-C_{3}\min\left(\frac{nz^{2}}{m\Phi_{2,0}^{4}},\frac{nz}{m\Phi_{2,0}^{2}}\right)\right]&\text{under~Assumption~\ref{assum:innov}~\ref{cond:dist}~\ref{cond:gauss}.}\end{array}\right.

Thanks to Lemma
F.1
, it follows that

\sup_{\omega}|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)-\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega))|_{\infty}=O_{P}(\vartheta_{n,p})
.
As for the bias term,
we apply the arguments analogous to those adopted in the proof of Proposition
F.9

and show
\sup_{\omega}|\mathsf{E}(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega))-\bm{\Sigma}_{x}(\omega)|_{\infty}=O(m^{-1})
,
which concludes the proof.
∎

The following three lemmas follow from Lemmas
F.6
–
F.8

with
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)
and
\mathbf{S}=\bm{\Sigma}_{\chi}(\omega)
,
as conditions (
F.6
)–(
F.9
) are met by
Assumption
2.1
and
Propositions
2.1
,
F.9
and
F.10
,
with the supremum over
\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]
taken where relevant.

Lemma F.11
.

Let
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)=[\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{x,j}(\omega),\,j=1,\ldots,q]
.
There exists a unitary, diagonal matrix

\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\in\mathbb{C}^{q\times q}
for each
\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}(\omega)}{p}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right\|_{F}=O_{P}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

Lemma F.12
.

Let
\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)=\text{diag}(\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega),\,j=1,\ldots,q)
.
Then,

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(q\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left\|\left(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)}{p}\right)^{-1}-\left(\frac{\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)}{p}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right).

Lemma F.13
.

Let
\bm{\varphi}_{i}
denote the
p
-vector whose
i
-th element is one
and the rest are set to be zero. Then with
\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)
defined in Lemma
F.11
,

(i)

uniformly for all
1\leq i\leq p
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)\right|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

(ii)

In addition,

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)\right|_{2}=O_{P}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

Lemma F.14
.

(i)

\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\max_{1\leq j\leq q}\mu_{\chi,j}^{1/2}(\omega)\max_{1\leq i\leq p}|e_{\chi,ij}(\omega)|=O(1)
.

(ii)

If
\psi_{n}\to 0
as
n,p\to\infty
, we have

\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\max_{1\leq j\leq q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}^{1/2}(\omega)|\widehat{e}_{x,ij}(\omega)|=O_{P}(1)
uniformly in
1\leq i\leq p
.

(iii)

If
\vartheta_{n,p}\to 0
as
n,p\to\infty
, we have

\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\max_{1\leq j\leq q}\widehat{\mu}_{\chi,j}^{1/2}(\omega)\max_{1\leq i\leq p}|\widehat{e}_{x,ij}(\omega)|=O_{P}(1)
.

Proof.

Note that by Assumption
F.1

(ii)
and
Lemma
F.3
,

\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)=\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)+\sigma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)

and
\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\sup_{\omega}\sigma_{x,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\leq B_{\sigma}<\infty
.
Then from that

\sigma_{\chi,ii}(\omega)=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mu_{\chi,j}(\omega)|e_{\chi,ij}(\omega)|^{2}\leq B_{\sigma}
,
the claim
(i)
follows. Next, by Proposition
F.9
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)|\widehat{e}_{x,ij}(\omega)|^{2}\leq\sup_{\omega}\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii}(\omega)\leq B_{\sigma}+O_{P}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\right)

uniformly over
1\leq i\leq p

and (
F.11
) indicates that
\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)\to\infty

as
p\to\infty
for
1\leq j\leq q
,
from which
(ii)
follows.
Also by Proposition
F.10
and Lemma
F.3
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\sup_{\omega}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)|\widehat{e}_{x,ij}(\omega)|^{2}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\sup_{\omega}\widehat{\sigma}_{x,ii}(\omega)\leq B_{\sigma}+O_{P}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\right)

which, combined with (
F.11
), leads to
(iii)
.
∎

Proposition F.15
.

(i)

Uniformly over
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|=O_{P}\left(q\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

(ii)

In addition,

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|=O_{P}\left(q\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

Proof.

First, note that

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|=\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\chi}(\omega)-\bm{\Sigma}_{\chi}(\omega)\right)\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left\{\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}^{*}(\omega)\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|\right.

\displaystyle+\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\left(\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)\right)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}^{*}(\omega)\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle+\left.\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)^{*}\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|\right\}=I+II+III.

By Lemmas
F.12
,
F.13

(i)
,

F.14

(i)
–
(ii)
and
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)\widehat{e}^{*}_{x,i^{\prime}j}\cdot\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\bm{\mathcal{O}}(\omega)\right)\right|_{2}\sqrt{\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\widehat{\mu}^{2}_{x,j}(\omega)|\widehat{e}_{x,i^{\prime}j}(\omega)|^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{p}\sum_{j=1}^{q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)}\right)=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{q}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right)

uniformly in
i
and
i^{\prime}
,
and
III
can be handled analogously.
By (
F.11
) and Lemma
F.14

(ii)
,

\displaystyle II\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\sqrt{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}(\omega)\right|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{p}\left\|\widehat{\bm{\mathcal{M}}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)-\bm{\mathcal{M}}_{\chi}(\omega)\right\|\cdot\sqrt{p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x,1:q}(\omega)\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right)\cdot\sup_{\omega}\frac{\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}{p^{2}}\sqrt{p\sum_{j=1}^{q}\frac{1}{\mu_{\chi,j}(\omega)}\cdot p\sum_{j=1}^{q}\frac{1}{\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}(\omega)}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O_{P}\left(q\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right)\right)

uniformly in
1\leq i\leq p
, which proves
(i)
.
As for
(ii)
, we take analogous steps
with Lemmas
F.13

(ii)
and
F.14

(iii)

replacing Lemmas
F.13

(i)
and
F.14

(ii)
, respectively.
∎

Proposition F.16
.

For
0\leq\ell\leq s
with some fixed
s\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

uniformly over
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

(ii)

In addition,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

Proof.

Note that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=\left|\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})e^{\iota\omega_{k}\ell}-\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)e^{\iota\omega\ell}d\omega\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})e^{\iota\omega_{k}\ell}-\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)e^{\iota\omega\ell}d\omega\right|=I+II.

By Proposition
F.15

(i)
,

\displaystyle I\leq 2\pi\sup_{\omega}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

Next, we can find
\{\omega_{k}^{*}\}_{k=-m}^{m-1}

and
\{\omega_{k}^{\circ}\}_{k=-m}^{m-1}

with
\omega_{k}^{*},\omega_{k}^{\circ}\in[\omega_{k},\omega_{k+1}]
,
such that

\displaystyle II\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m-1}\max_{\omega_{k}\leq\omega\leq\omega_{k+1}}\left|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})e^{\iota\omega_{k}\ell}-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)e^{\iota\omega\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m-1}\max_{\omega_{k}\leq\omega\leq\omega_{k+1}}\left|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{2\pi\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\sup_{\omega}|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)|}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m-1}\max_{\omega_{k}\leq\omega\leq\omega_{k+1}}\left|e^{\iota\omega_{k}\ell}-e^{\iota\omega\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m-1}\left(\left|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k}^{*})\right|+\left|\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k+1})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k}^{*})\right|\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\pi B_{\sigma}}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m-1}\left(\left|e^{\iota\omega_{k}\ell}-e^{\iota\omega_{k}^{\circ}\ell}\right|+\left|e^{\iota\omega_{k+1}\ell}-e^{\iota\omega_{k}^{\circ}\ell}\right|\right)=III+IV,

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
F.3
.
Then by Lemma
F.4
,
the functions
\omega\mapsto\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega)
possess derivatives of any order and are of bounded variation such that
III=O(m^{-1})
.
Also from the finiteness of
s
(Assumption
B.2

(i)
)
and as the exponential function has bounded variation,

IV=O(m^{-1})
uniformly in
0\leq\ell\leq s
, which completes the proof of
(i)
.
As for
(ii)
, by Proposition
F.15

(ii)
,

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\widehat{\sigma}_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})-\sigma_{\chi,ii^{\prime}}(\omega_{k})\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right),

which replaces the above
I
. Since
II
is deterministic and the bound derived above is uniform over
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
,
the proof is complete.
∎

F.2.3
Proof of Proposition
3.2

Proposition
3.2
is a direct consequence of Corollary
F.17
.

Corollary F.17
.

For
0\leq\ell\leq d
, the following statements hold.

(i)

Uniformly over
1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\psi_{n}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

(ii)

In addition,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\widehat{\gamma}_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)-\gamma_{\xi,ii^{\prime}}(\ell)\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{qp^{2}}{\inf_{\omega\in[-\pi,\pi]}\mu_{\chi,q}^{2}(\omega)}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

Proof.

Noting that
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\xi}(\omega)=\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)-\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\chi}(\omega)

by Assumption
F.1

(ii)
,
the result in
(i)
is an immediate consequence of
Lemma
F.5

(i)
(see also (
F.5
)) and Proposition
F.16

(i)
.
Similarly,
(ii)
follows from
Lemma
F.5

(ii)
and Proposition
F.16

(ii)
.
∎

F.2.4
Proof of Proposition
3.3

Throughout, we refer to the estimator obtained as in (
8
) as
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}
and the tuning parameter by
\lambda^{\text{{las}}}
.

The problem in (
8
) can be solved in parallel as

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}={\arg\min}_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{pd}}\ \mathbf{m}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{m}-2\mathbf{m}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}+\lambda^{\text{{las}}}|\mathbf{m}|_{1},

with
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}=[\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j},\,1\leq j\leq p]
.

Throughout the proof, we condition our arguments on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
.
We first establish the followings:

(C1)

\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}
is positive semi-definite.

(C2)

Lower restricted eigenvalue:

\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}
satisfies
\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}\geq\kappa|\mathbf{v}|^{2}-\tau|\mathbf{v}|_{1}^{2}

for all
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{dp}
, with curvature
\kappa=2\pi m_{\xi}
and tolerance
\tau=C_{\xi}(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})
.

(C3)

Deviation bound:

|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\bm{\beta}|_{\infty}\leq C_{\xi}(\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}+1)(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})
.

To see
(C1)
, note that for any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}^{\top}(2\pi\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega))\mathbf{a}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=-m}^{m}K\left(\frac{\ell}{m}\right)\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(\ell)\exp(-\iota\ell\omega)=\mathbf{e}(\omega)^{*}\begin{bmatrix}\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(0)&\ldots&\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(m-1)\\
&\ddots&\\
\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(-m+1)&\ldots&\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(0)\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{e}(\omega),

where
\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(\ell)=n^{-1}\sum_{t=\ell+1}^{n}\mathbf{(}a^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{t-\ell})(\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{t})

and
\mathbf{e}(\omega)=(e^{-\iota\omega\ell}/\sqrt{m},\,1\leq\ell\leq m)^{\top}
.
From the positive semi-definiteness of the sample autocovariance function
\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathbf{a}}(\cdot)

(see e.g.
McLeod and Jimenéz, 1984
), that of
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{x}(\omega)
follows.
Then,
\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\xi}(\omega)=\sum_{j>q}\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{x,j}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{x,j}^{*}

is also positive semi-definite.
Noting that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}=\frac{2\pi}{2m+1}\sum_{k=-m}^{m}\begin{bmatrix}e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 1}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(d-1)}\\
e^{\iota\omega_{k}(-1)}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(d-2)}\\
&&\ddots&\\
e^{\iota\omega_{k}(1-d)}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(2-d)}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}\end{bmatrix}\otimes\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\xi}(\omega_{k}),

where
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product, and that for all
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{C}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{a}^{*}\begin{bmatrix}e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 1}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(d-1)}\\
e^{\iota\omega_{k}(-1)}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(d-2)}\\
&&\ddots&\\
e^{\iota\omega_{k}(1-d)}&e^{\iota\omega_{k}(2-d)}&\ldots&e^{\iota\omega_{k}\cdot 0}\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{a}=\left|\sum_{j=1}^{d}a_{j}e^{-\iota\omega_{k}j}\right|^{2}\geq 0,

we have
\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}
positive semi-definite.

To see
(C2)
, conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left(\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}-\mathbbm{G}\right|_{\infty},\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\mathbbm{g}\right|_{\infty}\right)=\max_{0\leq\ell\leq d}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(\ell)-\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(\ell)\right|_{\infty}\leq C_{\xi}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right).

(F.17)

Then, uniformly over
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{dp}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}\geq\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbbm{G}\mathbf{v}-\left|\mathbf{v}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}-\mathbbm{G})\mathbf{v}\right|\geq\Lambda_{\min}(\mathbbm{G})|\mathbf{v}|_{2}^{2}-\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}-\mathbbm{G}\right|_{\infty}|\mathbf{v}|_{1}^{2}

such that on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
, we can set

\kappa=\Lambda_{\min}(\mathbbm{G})\geq 2\pi m_{\xi}
and

\tau=C_{\xi}(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})
.
Also from (
F.17
), we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\bm{\beta}|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\mathbbm{g}|_{\infty}+|(\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}-\mathbbm{G})\bm{\beta}|_{\infty}\leq\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\mathbbm{g}\right|_{\infty}+\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}-\mathbbm{G}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\xi}(\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}+1)\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)

such that
(C3)
follows.

Denote by
\mathbf{v}=\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}
and for any vector
\mathbf{a}=(a_{i},\,1\leq i\leq p)^{\top}
and a set
\mathcal{A}\subset\{1,\ldots,p\}
, we write
\mathbf{a}_{\mathcal{A}}=(a_{i}\mathbb{I}_{\{i\in\mathcal{A}\}},1\leq i\leq p)^{\top}
.
Also, let
\mathcal{S}_{j}
denote the support of
\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}
such that
|\mathcal{S}_{j}|=s_{0,j}
.
Then by construction,

\displaystyle(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-2(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}+\lambda^{\text{{las}}}|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}-2\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}+\lambda^{\text{{las}}}|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1},\text{ \ such that}

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}\leq\lambda^{\text{{las}}}\left(|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}-|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\right)-2\mathbf{v}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\lambda^{\text{{las}}}\left(|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}-|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}+\mathbf{v}|_{1}\right)+\frac{\lambda^{\text{{las}}}}{2}|\mathbf{v}|_{1}\leq\lambda^{\text{{las}}}\left(|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}-|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{c}}|_{1}\right)+\frac{\lambda^{\text{{las}}}}{2}|\mathbf{v}|_{1},

(F.18)

where the second inequality follows from
(C3)
and the choice of
\lambda^{\text{{las}}}
, and the last from that

\displaystyle|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}+\mathbf{v}|_{1}=|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}+\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}+|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{c}}|_{1}\geq|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}-|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}+|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{c}}|_{1}.

From the above and
(C1)
, it follows that

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}\leq\frac{\lambda^{\text{{las}}}}{2}\left(3|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}-|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{c}}|_{1}\right),

\displaystyle\text{i.e. \ }|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}^{c}}|_{1}\leq 3|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}\text{ \ and \ }|\mathbf{v}|_{1}\leq 4|\mathbf{v}_{\mathcal{S}_{j}}|_{1}\leq 4\sqrt{s_{0,j}}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}.

Combining these observations with
(C2)
and (
F.18
), we have

\displaystyle 6\lambda^{\text{{las}}}\sqrt{s_{0,j}}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\pi m_{\xi}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}^{2}-C_{\xi}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)|\mathbf{v}|_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 2\pi m_{\xi}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}^{2}\left(1-\frac{8C_{\xi}s_{0,j}(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})}{\pi m_{\xi}}\right)\geq\pi m_{\xi}|\mathbf{v}|_{2}^{2}

from the condition imposed on
s_{\text{in}}\geq s_{0,j}
.
Hence, for all
1\leq j\leq p
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{2}\leq\frac{6\sqrt{s_{0,j}}\lambda^{\text{{las}}}}{\pi m_{\xi}},\quad\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}\leq\frac{24s_{0,j}\lambda^{\text{{las}}}}{\pi m_{\xi}}.

Besides, by Karush-Kuhn-Tucker conditions, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{0}\in\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}+\lambda^{\text{{las}}}\mathrm{sgn}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j})

where, defined as

\displaystyle\mathrm{sgn}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }x>0,\\
-1&\text{ if }x<0,\\
[-1,1]&\text{ if }x=0,\end{cases}

\mathrm{sgn}(\cdot)
acts element-wise. It implies that
\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}\right|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{\text{{las}}}
.
Also noting that from (
F.18
), we have

\displaystyle 0\leq|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}-|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}+|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\right),\text{ \ i.e. \ }|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq 3|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}.

Therefore, we obtain from
(C3)
,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}=\left|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\left(\mathbbm{G}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}+\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}+\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}-\mathbbm{g}_{\cdot j}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\right\|_{1}\left(\left|\mathbbm{G}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\right|_{\infty}\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}+\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}-\mathbbm{g}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}\right)\leq 4\left\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\right\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{las}}}

which, together with the trivial bound
|\mathbf{v}|_{\infty}\leq|\mathbf{v}|_{2}
, proves the final claim.

F.2.5
Proof of Proposition
A.1

As in Appendix
F.2.4
, we condition our arguments on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
.
Note that solving (
A.1
) is equivalent to solving

\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}={\arg\min}_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{pd}}|\mathbf{m}|_{1}\quad\text{subject to}\quad\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{m}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}\leq\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}\quad\text{for }1\leq j\leq p,

(see e.g. Lemma 1 of
Cai et al., 2011
).
Then,
\bm{\beta}
is a feasible solution to (
A.1
) from
(C3)
,
and therefore
|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}

for all
1\leq j\leq p
.
Also, writing
\mathbf{v}=\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}\leq|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\mathbf{v}|_{\infty}|\mathbf{v}|_{1}\leq 2\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}|\mathbf{v}|_{1}.

Then using
(C1)
–
(C3)
and the arguments analogous to those adopted in Appendix
F.2.4
, we obtain the results with slightly different multiplicative constants.
In particular,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}=\left|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\left(\mathbbm{G}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}+\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}+\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}-\mathbbm{g}_{\cdot j}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\right\|_{1}\left(\left|\mathbbm{G}-\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\right|_{\infty}\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}+\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{G}}\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}_{\cdot j}-\mathbbm{g}_{\cdot j}\right|_{\infty}\right)\leq 2\left\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\right\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}

(F.19)

for all
1\leq j\leq p
.
In fact, without requiring exact sparsity, we can control the estimation error in
\ell_{1}
-norm.
Let
\widetilde{\lambda}\in(0,\infty)
be a threshold level to be defined later, and denote by

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{pd}\min\left(\frac{|\beta_{ij}|}{\widetilde{\lambda}},1\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}=\left\{1\leq i\leq pd:\,|\beta_{ij}|>\widetilde{\lambda}\right\}.

By definition, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}_{j}=|\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}|+\sum_{i\in\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{c}_{j}}\frac{|\beta_{ij}|}{\widetilde{\lambda}}\geq|\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}|.

(F.20)

Then for all
1\leq j\leq p
,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}\leq\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{c},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{c},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}-\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}=\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}-\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{c},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}-\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}-\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}^{c},j}\right|_{1}+\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}-\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}\leq 2\left|\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}^{c}_{j},j}\right|_{1}+2\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}-\bm{\beta}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j},j}\right|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\widetilde{\lambda}\widetilde{s}_{j}+4|\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}_{j}|\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}\leq 2\widetilde{s}_{j}(\widetilde{\lambda}+2\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}),

where the second inequality follows from that
|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq|\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}
,
the third from the triangular inequality,
the fourth from (
F.19
),
and the last from (
F.20
).
Setting
\widetilde{\lambda}=\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}
, by the definition of
s_{0,j}(\varrho)
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}\widetilde{s}_{j}\leq\widetilde{\lambda}\sum_{i=1}^{pd}\min\left(\frac{|\beta_{ij}|^{\varrho}}{\widetilde{\lambda}^{\varrho}},1\right)\leq s_{0,j}(\varrho)\widetilde{\lambda}^{1-\varrho}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}-\bm{\beta}\right|_{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\beta}_{\cdot j}\right|_{1}\leq 6\sum_{j=1}^{p}s_{0,j}(\varrho)\widetilde{\lambda}^{1-\rho}=6s_{0}(\varrho)\left(\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}\right)^{1-\varrho}.

F.2.6
Proof of Proposition
3.5

(i)

We prove the following proposition from which Proposition
3.5

(i)
follows.
Throughout, we refer to the estimator obtained as in (
8
) as
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}
.

Proposition F.18
.

(i)

Suppose that Assumption
2.3
is met
and the estimator
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
fulfils

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}\right\|_{1}\leq C_{\beta}s_{\text{in}}\zeta_{n,p}

(F.21)

with some constant
C_{\beta}>0
.
Then on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
defined in (
11
),
there exists a large enough constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right|_{\infty}\leq C\phi_{n,p}\quad\text{with}\quad\phi_{n,p}=\left(s_{\text{in}}\zeta_{n,p}\vee\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

(ii)

Further, suppose that
\bm{\Delta}
belongs to the parameter space

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}(\varrho,s_{\delta}(\varrho))=\left\{\mathbf{M}=[m_{ii^{\prime}}]\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:\,\mathbf{M}\text{ is positive definite},\,\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{p}|m_{ii^{\prime}}|^{\varrho}\leq s_{\delta}(\varrho)\right\}

for some
\varrho\in[0,1)
,
and set
\eta=C\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\phi_{n,p}
.
Then on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right|_{\infty}\leq 4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right\|\leq 12s_{\delta}(\varrho)\left(4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)^{1-\varrho}.

Remark F.2
.

From the proof of Proposition
3.3
and
A.1
, both
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{las}}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\text{{DS}}}
satisfy (
F.21
) with
\zeta_{n,p}=\lambda^{\text{{las}}}
and
\zeta_{n,p}=\|\mathbbm{G}^{-1}\|_{1}\lambda^{\text{{DS}}}
, respectively.
Then,
\phi_{n,p}
involved in the error bound in Proposition
F.18

(i)
reduces to
\phi_{n,p}=s_{\text{in}}\zeta_{n,p}
.

Proof of Proposition
F.18

(i)
.

We write
\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\mathbf{A}_{\ell}\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(\ell)=\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbbm{g}

and
\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\ell}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(\ell)=\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}
.
Then on
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
,
we have
|\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}|_{\infty}\leq\max_{0\leq\ell\leq d}|\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(\ell)|_{\infty}+C_{\xi}(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})\leq C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}

for a large enough
C_{\Xi,\varsigma,\varepsilon}>0
and
by Lemma
F.2
.
Also,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(0)-\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(0)\right|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\bm{\beta}^{\top}\mathbbm{g}\right|_{\infty}=:I+II,

where
I\leq C_{\xi}(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee m^{-1}\vee p^{-1/2})
and

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}\right)^{\top}\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\bm{\beta}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbbm{g}}-\mathbbm{g}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}\right\|_{1}\;\max_{0\leq\ell\leq d}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(\ell)\right|_{\infty}+\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}\;\max_{0\leq\ell\leq d}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(\ell)-\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(\ell)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(s_{\text{in}}\zeta_{n,p}+\|\bm{\beta}\|_{1}\left(\vartheta_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{m}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right)

for large enough constant
C>0
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
F.18

(ii)
.

The proof takes analogous steps as those in the proof of Theorem 6 of
Cai et al., 2011
.
On
\mathcal{E}_{n,p}
, by Proposition
F.18

(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\bm{\Delta}-\mathbf{I}\right|_{\infty}\leq\left|\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right)\bm{\Delta}\right|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right|_{\infty}\leq\eta=C\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\phi_{n,p},

and thus
\bm{\Delta}
is a feasible solution to (
9
).
Since solving the problem in (
9
) is equivalent to solving

\displaystyle\check{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}={\arg\min}_{\mathbf{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}|\mathbf{m}|_{1}\quad\text{subject to}\quad\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\mathbf{m}-\bm{\varphi}_{j}\right|_{\infty}\leq\eta

for all
1\leq j\leq p
, it follows that

\|\check{\bm{\Delta}}\|_{1}=\max_{1\leq j\leq p}|\check{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\left(\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\mathbf{I}\right|_{\infty}+\left|\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right)\bm{\Delta}\right|_{\infty}\leq\eta+\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right|_{\infty}\leq 2\eta,

\displaystyle\therefore\,\left|\bm{\Gamma}\left(\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\left(\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\right|_{\infty}+\left|\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right)\left(\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta+\left\|\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{1}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}-\bm{\Gamma}\right|_{\infty}\leq 4\eta.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right|_{\infty}\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{1}\;\left|\bm{\Gamma}\left(\check{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\right|_{\infty}\leq 4\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{1}\eta,

and from the definition of
\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}
,
the bound on
|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}|_{\infty}
follows.

Next, let
\varpi=|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}|_{\infty}

and define
\mathbf{h}_{j}=\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}-\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}
,

\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}=(\widehat{\delta}_{ij}\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}},\,1\leq i\leq p)^{\top}-\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}

and
\mathbf{h}^{(2)}_{j}=\mathbf{h}_{j}-\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}
.
By definition, we have
|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq|\check{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq|\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}
for all
1\leq j\leq p
.
Then,

\displaystyle|\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}|_{1}-|\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}|_{1}+|\mathbf{h}^{(2)}_{j}|_{1}\leq|\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}+\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}|_{1}+|\mathbf{h}^{(2)}_{j}|_{1}=|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}_{\cdot j}|_{1}\leq|\bm{\Delta}_{\cdot j}|_{1},

which implies that
|\mathbf{h}^{(2)}_{j}|_{1}\leq|\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}|_{1}

and thus
|\mathbf{h}_{j}|_{1}\leq 2|\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}|_{1}
.
The latter is bounded as

\displaystyle|\mathbf{h}^{(1)}_{j}|_{1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}-\delta_{ij}\right|_{1}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left|\delta_{ij}\mathbb{I}_{\{|\delta_{ij}|<2\varpi\}}\right|_{1}+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left|\left(\widehat{\delta}_{ij}-\delta_{ij}\right)\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}+\delta_{ij}\left(\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{|\delta_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}\right)\right|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle s_{\delta}(\varrho)(2\varpi)^{1-\varrho}+\varpi\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\delta_{ij}|\left|\mathbb{I}_{\{|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}-\mathbb{I}_{\{|\delta_{ij}|\geq 2\varpi\}}\right|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle s_{\delta}(\varrho)(2\varpi)^{1-\varrho}+\varpi\sum_{i=1}^{p}\mathbb{I}_{\{|\delta_{ij}|\geq\varpi\}}+\sum_{i=1}^{p}|\delta_{ij}|\mathbb{I}_{\{||\delta_{ij}|-2\varpi|\leq|\widehat{\delta}_{ij}-\delta_{ij}|\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle s_{\delta}(\varrho)\varpi^{1-\varrho}\left(1+2^{1-\varrho}+3^{1-\varrho}\right),

from which we drive that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right\|\leq\left\|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{1}\leq 12s_{\delta}(\varrho)\varpi^{1-\varrho}.

∎

F.2.7
Proof of Proposition
3.5

(ii)

In what follows, we omit the dependence of
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\ell}
on
\mathfrak{t}
.
Write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi}\left|\widehat{\bm{\Omega}}-\bm{\Omega}\right|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right)^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}\mathcal{A}(1)\right|_{\infty}+\left|\mathcal{A}^{\top}(1)\left(\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}\right)\mathcal{A}(1)\right|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\top}(1)\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right)\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=I+II+III.

Note that from Corollaries
3.4
and
A.2
and the conditions of the proposition,

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq p}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\left|\widehat{A}_{\xi,\ell,ij}-A_{\ell,ij}\right|_{1}\leq s_{\text{out}}\mathfrak{t},

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1}+\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\left\|\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1},

\displaystyle|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq|\bm{\Delta}|_{\infty}+|\widehat{\bm{\Delta}}-\bm{\Delta}|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta.

Also, for any compatible matrices
\mathbf{A}
,
\mathbf{B}
and
\mathbf{C}
,
we have
|\mathbf{A}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathbf{A}\|_{\infty}|\mathbf{B}|_{\infty}\|\mathbf{C}\|_{1}
.
Then,

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1}\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\leq\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)s_{\text{out}}\mathfrak{t},

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\leq 4\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}^{2}\;\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta,

\displaystyle III

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)\left\|\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(1)-\mathcal{A}(1)\right\|_{1}\leq 2\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)s_{\text{out}}\mathfrak{t},

and therefore

\displaystyle I+II+III\leq

\displaystyle\,\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\left[3\left(\|\bm{\Delta}\|+4\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right)s_{\text{out}}\mathfrak{t}+4\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\left(3\|\bm{\Delta}\|s_{\text{out}}\mathfrak{t}+16\|\mathcal{A}(1)\|_{1}\|\bm{\Delta}\|_{1}\eta\right),

which concludes the proof.

F.2.8
Proof of Remark
3.2

In this section, we operate under the restricted GDFM in Section
4.1

and use the notations given therein.
The proofs arguments are analogous to those adopted in the proof of Proposition
F.15

(ii)
.

With
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}=\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(0)
and
\mathbf{S}=\bm{\Gamma}_{\chi}(0)
,
the conditions (
F.6
)–(
F.9
) are met by
Assumption
4.1

(ii)
, Propositions
2.1
and
F.5

with
\zeta_{n,p}=1/\sqrt{n}
and
\bar{\zeta}_{n,p}=\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}
.
Then applying Lemmas
F.6
–
F.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\max_{1\leq j\leq r}|\widehat{\mu}_{x,j}-\mu_{\chi,j}|

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\frac{1}{p}\right),

\displaystyle\sqrt{p}\cdot\frac{\mu_{\chi,r}^{2}}{p^{2}}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)

with some unitary, diagonal matrix
\bm{\mathcal{O}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
.
Also using the arguments in the proof of Lemma
F.14
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq r}\mu_{\chi,j}^{1/2}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}|e_{\chi,ij}|=O(1)\quad\text{and}\quad\max_{1\leq j\leq r}\widehat{\mu}_{\chi,j}^{1/2}\max_{1\leq i\leq p}|\widehat{e}_{x,ij}|=O_{P}(1).

(F.22)

Noting that
\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\chi}(h)=\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(h)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}

and that
\bm{\Gamma}_{\chi}(h)=\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\chi}(h)\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{\xi}(h)-\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(h)\right|_{\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\left|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(h)-\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(h)\right|_{\infty}+\left|\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(h)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(h)\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+\left|\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(h)\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\right|_{\infty}=I+II+III,

where
I=O_{P}(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee p^{-1/2})
from Lemma
F.5
.
As for
III
, we have

\displaystyle III=\max_{1\leq i,i^{\prime}\leq p}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{\xi}(h)\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|=O_{P}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\chi,r}}\right)

from (
F.22
) and Proposition
2.1
. Next,

\displaystyle II\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\right)\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(h)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|+\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}_{x}(h)-\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(h)\right)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|

\displaystyle+\max_{i,i^{\prime}}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}_{x}(h)\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\right)\bm{\varphi}_{i^{\prime}}\right|=IV+V+VI.

Denoting the diagonal elements of
\bm{\mathcal{O}}
by
O_{jj},\,1\leq j\leq r
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{i}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\right|_{2}\leq\max_{i}\sqrt{\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{p}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}(\widehat{e}_{x,ij}-O_{jj}e_{\chi,ij})\widehat{e}_{x,i^{\prime}j}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\max_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{r}(\widehat{e}_{x,ij}-O_{jj}e_{\chi,ij})^{2}\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{p}\widehat{e}_{x,i^{\prime}j}^{2}}=O_{P}\left(\frac{rp^{3/2}}{\mu_{\chi,r}^{2}}\left(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right)

such that

\displaystyle\max_{i}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}^{\top}\right)\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}^{\top}\right|_{2}+\max_{i}\left|\bm{\varphi}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{E}}_{x}-\mathbf{E}_{\chi}\bm{\mathcal{O}}\right)^{\top}\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=O_{P}\left(\frac{rp^{3/2}}{\mu_{\chi,r}^{2}}\left(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right).

From this, (
F.22
) and Lemma
F.5

(i)
, we have

\displaystyle IV=O_{P}\left(\frac{rp^{3/2}}{\mu_{\chi,r}^{2}}\left(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\cdot\frac{p}{\sqrt{\mu_{\chi,r}}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad V=O_{P}\left(\frac{rp}{\mu_{x,r}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\vee\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\right)

(assuming that
(p/\mu_{\chi,r})^{-5/2}(\widetilde{\vartheta}_{n,p}\vee p^{-1/2})=o(1)
), and
VI
is similarly handled as
IV
.
Finally, assuming that the factor strengths are
\varrho_{1}=\ldots=\varrho_{r}=1

(in Assumption
4.1

(ii)
), the conclusion follows.

Results in Section

and Appendix
